\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}^{\prime}_{1:h-1}).

(C.2)

We prove this case by case. If
h=2t
with
t\in[H]
, then from the result of Theorem
IV.5
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\dagger}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\dagger}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}^{\prime}_{1:h-1}).

Therefore, now we consider the case that
h=2t-1
with
t\in[H]
.

Suppose
h_{1}
is odd, which means that
\overline{a}_{h_{1}}
corresponds to the communication action in
\mathcal{L}
. Then, it holds that
\overline{c}_{h_{1}}\subseteq\overline{c}_{h},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}=m_{i_{1},\frac{h_{1}+1}{2}}\in\overline{c}_{h}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h_{1}},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h_{1}},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}\backslash\overline{g}_{i_{1},h_{1}})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}^{\prime}),

where the second equality is because the input and output of
\overline{g}_{i_{1},h_{1}}
are
\overline{c}_{h_{1}}
and
\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}
.

Suppose
h_{1}
is even, which means that
h_{1}
is a control timestep, and let
t_{1}=\frac{h_{1}}{2}
. If
\sigma(\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\subseteq\sigma(\overline{c}_{h})
and
\sigma(\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\subseteq\sigma(\overline{c}_{h})
, then there exist deterministic measurable functions
\overline{\alpha}_{1},\overline{\alpha}_{2}
such that
\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}}=\overline{\alpha}_{1}(\overline{c}_{h}),\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}=\overline{\alpha}_{2}(\overline{c}_{h})
, and further it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{\alpha}_{1}(\overline{c}_{h}),\overline{\alpha}_{2}(\overline{c}_{h}),\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}^{\prime}).

If
\sigma(\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\nsubseteq\sigma(\overline{c}_{h})
or
\sigma(\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\nsubseteq\sigma(\overline{c}_{h})
. Since
h_{1}
is even,
\overline{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}}=\breve{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}},\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}=\breve{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}
. Also, we know
\overline{c}_{h}=\breve{c}_{h}
, then it holds that
\sigma(\breve{\tau}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\nsubseteq\sigma(\breve{c}_{h})
or
\sigma(\breve{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}})\nsubseteq\sigma(\breve{c}_{h})
.
Firstly, from the sQC of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\dagger}
, we know that agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence agent
(i_{2},h)
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\dagger}
for any
i_{2}\neq i_{1}
. Then, as shown in the proof of Theorem
IV.5
, we know that agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\breve{s}_{h_{1}+1}=\overline{s}_{h_{1}+1}
and does not influence
\breve{c}_{h^{\prime}}=\overline{c}_{h^{\prime}}
for any
h^{\prime}\leq h
. Secondly, from Assumption
II.1
, we know that for any
i\in[n],p_{i,(t_{1}+1)^{-}}=\xi_{i,t_{1}+1}(p_{i,t_{1}^{+}},a_{i,t_{1}},o_{i,t_{1}+1})
, where
\xi_{i,t_{1}+1}
is a fixed transformation. Also, from Assumption
III.5
, we know that
a_{i,t_{1}}\notin p_{i,(t_{1}+1)^{-}}
. Therefore, we can write
p_{i,(t_{1}+1)^{-}}=\xi_{i,t_{1}+1}(p_{i,t_{1}^{+}},o_{i,t_{1}+1})
. From the definition of refinement, we know that
\overline{p}_{i,h_{1}+1}=\xi_{i,t_{1}+1}(p_{i,t_{1}^{+}},\overline{o}_{i,h_{1}+1})
. Since agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{s}_{h_{1}+1}
, it does not influence
\overline{o}_{i,h_{1}+1}
. Also, agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{c}_{h_{1}+1}
and
p_{i,h_{1}^{+}}
(which happens before choosing
a_{i_{1},t_{1}}=\overline{a}_{i_{1},h_{1}}
). Therefore, agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{\tau}_{i,h_{1}+1}
, and thus does not influence
\overline{a}_{i,h_{1}+1}
for any
i\in[n]
. Thirdly, we know that for any
i\in[n],\overline{p}_{i,h_{1}+2}=\overline{\xi}_{i,h_{1}+2}(\overline{p}_{i,h_{1}+1},\overline{a}_{i,h_{1}+1},\overline{o}_{i,h_{1}+2})
, where
\overline{o}_{i,h_{1}+2}=\emptyset
. Since agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{p}_{i,h_{1}+1}
and
\overline{a}_{i,h_{1}+1}
, it does not influence
\overline{p}_{i,h_{1}+2}
. Also, we know that it does not influence
\overline{\tau}_{i,h_{1}+2}
. Therefore, agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{\tau}_{i,h_{1}+2}
, and thus does not influence
\overline{a}_{i,h_{1}+2}
. This way, we know that agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{\tau}_{i,h^{\prime}}
for any
i\in[n],h_{1}<h^{\prime}\leq h
.

Finally, since we proved that agent
(i_{1},h_{1})
does not influence
\overline{s}_{h_{1}+1}
, and does not influence
\overline{\tau}_{i,h^{\prime}},\forall i\in[n],h_{1}\leq h^{\prime}<h
, then it does not influence
\overline{a}_{i,h^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h},\overline{c}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{\tau}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\{\overline{\tau}_{i,h}\}_{i\in[n]}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\{\overline{\tau}_{i,h}\}_{i\in[n]}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}^{\prime})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:h-1}^{\prime}),

which completes the proof.
∎

C-G
Important Auxiliary Definitions

Definition C.3

(Full-history strategy)
.

Given any Dec-POMDP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
constructed from an LTC
\mathcal{L}
after reformulation, strict expansion, and refinement. We define the strategy
g_{h}:\prodop\displaylimits_{t=1}^{h}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{t}\times\prodop\displaylimits_{t=1}^{h-1}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}
that uses the full joint observation-action history as a
full-history strategy
, and define
\mathcal{G}_{h}
as the space of all such possible
g_{h}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{G}_{h}=\{g_{h}:\prodop\displaylimits_{t=1}^{h}\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{t}\times\prodop\displaylimits_{t=1}^{h-1}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}\}
.

Definition C.4

(Perfect recall
[
18
]
)
.

We say that agent
i
has perfect recall if
\forall h=2,\cdots,\overline{H}
,
it holds that
\overline{\tau}_{i,h-1}\cup\{\overline{a}_{i,h-1}\}\subseteq\overline{\tau}_{i,h}.

If for any
i\in[n]
, agent
i
has perfect recall, we call that the Dec-POMDP has a perfect recall property.

The following definitions are important for solving the Dec-POMDP constructed from LTC.

Definition C.5

(Value function)
.

For each
i\in[n]
and
h\in[\overline{H}]
, given common information
\overline{c}_{h}
and strategy
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
, the value function conditioned on the common information is defined as:

V^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h}):=\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h^{\prime}=h}^{\overline{H}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{h^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{a}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{p}_{h^{\prime}}){\,\bigg|\,}\overline{c}_{h}\right],

(C.3)

where
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{h^{\prime}}
takes
\overline{s}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{a}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{p}_{h^{\prime}}
as input.

Definition C.6

(Prescription and Q-value function)
.

Prescription is an important concept in the common-information-based framework
[
28
,
27
]
. For any
h\in[\overline{H}],i\in[n]
, the prescription of agent
i
at the timestep
h
is defined as
\gamma_{i,h}\in\Gamma_{i,h}
, where
\Gamma_{i,h}:=\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{i,h}=\mathcal{M}_{i,\frac{h+1}{2}}
if
h=2k-1,k\in[H]
, and
\Gamma_{i,h}:=\{\gamma_{i,h}:\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{i,h}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{i,h}\}
if
h=2k,k\in[H]
. We use
\gamma_{h}:=(\gamma_{1,h},\cdots,\gamma_{n,h})
to denote the joint prescription and
\Gamma_{h}
to denote the joint prescription space. The prescriptions are the marginalization of the strategies
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}
, i.e.,
\forall k\in[H],\gamma_{i,2k-1}=\overline{g}_{i,2k-1}(\overline{c}_{2k-1}),\gamma_{i,2k}(\cdot)=\overline{g}_{i,2k}(\overline{c}_{2k},\cdot)
. Then, for any
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}
, we can define the Q-value function as

Q^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}):=\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}\left[\sumop\displaylimits_{h^{\prime}=h}^{\overline{H}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{h^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{a}_{h^{\prime}},\overline{p}_{h^{\prime}}){\,\bigg|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}\right].

(C.4)

Note that for any
i\in[n]
, and any odd timestep
2k-1
with
k\in[H]
, by Assumption
III.4
, we consider the strategies that only take common information as input. Thus, the prescription
\gamma_{i,2k-1}=\overline{a}_{i,2k-1}
. To unify the notation, we may also write it as
\gamma_{i,2k-1}(\cdot)=\overline{g}_{i,2k-1}(\overline{c}_{2k-1},\cdot)
, where
\cdot
takes the value of
\emptyset
rather than
\overline{p}_{i,2k-1}
.

Definition C.7

(Expected approximate common information model)
.

We define an
expected approximate common information model
of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
as

\mathcal{M}:=\left(\{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\{\widehat{\phi}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\{\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{M},z}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\Gamma,\{\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}\right)

where
\Gamma=\{\Gamma_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
is the joint prescription space,
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}
is the space of approximate common information at timestep
h
.
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{M},z}_{h}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\times\Gamma_{h}\rightarrow\Delta(\overline{Z}_{h+1})
gives the probability of
\overline{z}_{h+1}
under
\widehat{c}_{h}
and
\gamma_{h}
.
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}:\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\times\Gamma_{h}\rightarrow[0,1]
gives the reward at timestep
h
given
\widehat{c}_{h}
and
\gamma_{h}
. Then, we call that
\mathcal{M}
is an
(\epsilon_{r}(\mathcal{M}),\epsilon_{z}(\mathcal{M}))
-expected-approximate common information model
of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
, if it has some compression function Compress
h
such that
\widehat{c}_{h}=
Compress
{}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h})
for each
h\in[\overline{H}]
, and satisfies the following:

•

There exists a transformation function
\widehat{\phi}_{h}
such that

\widehat{c}_{h}=\widehat{\phi}_{h}(\widehat{c}_{h-1},\overline{z}_{h}),\qquad\text{ where $\overline{z}_{h}=\overline{c}_{h}\backslash\overline{c}_{h-1}$}.

(C.5)

•

For any
\overline{g}_{1:h-1}
and any prescription
\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{1:h}\sim\overline{g}_{1:h-1}}|\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}){\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}]-\widehat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})|\leq\epsilon_{r}(\mathcal{M}).

(C.6)

•

For any
\overline{g}_{1:h-1}
and any prescription
\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{1:h}\sim\overline{g}_{1:h-1}}||\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{M},z}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})||_{1}\leq\epsilon_{z}(\mathcal{M}).

(C.7)

Definition C.8

(Value functions under
\mathcal{M}
)
.

Given a Dec-POMDP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
and its expected approximate common information model
\mathcal{M}
. For any strategy
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}},h\in[\overline{H}]
, we define the value function and Q-value functions under
\mathcal{M}
as

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\text{Compress}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h}),\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{M}}[V_{h+1}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h+1}){\,|\,}\text{Compress}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h}),\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]}],

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\text{Compress}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h}),\gamma_{h})+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{M}}[V_{h+1}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h+1}){\,|\,}\text{Compress}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h}),\gamma_{h}],

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{\ast,\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})=\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}Q_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}).

Definition C.9

(Model-belief consistency)
.

We say the expected approximate common information model
\mathcal{M}
of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
is
consistent with
some approximate common-information-based beliefs
\{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
, if it satisfies the following: for all
i\in[n]
,
h\in H
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2h-1}^{\mathcal{M},z}(\overline{z}_{2h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h-1},{\gamma}_{2h-1})=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{2h-1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{p}_{2h-1}:\\
\overline{\chi}_{2h}(\overline{p}_{2h-1},\gamma_{2h-1},\overline{o}_{2h}=\emptyset)=\overline{z}_{2h}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{2h-1}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2h-1},\overline{p}_{2h-1}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h-1}),

(C.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{2h}^{\mathcal{M},z}(\overline{z}_{2h+1}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h},{\gamma}_{2h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{2h}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\overline{p}_{2h},\overline{a}_{2h},\overline{o}_{2h+1}:\\
\overline{\chi}_{2h+1}(\overline{p}_{2h},\overline{a}_{2h},\overline{o}_{2h+1})=\overline{z}_{2h+1}\end{subarray}}\Big(\mathbb{P}_{2h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2h},\overline{p}_{2h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h})\mathds{1}[\overline{a}_{2h}=\gamma_{2h}(\overline{p}_{2h})]

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{2h+1}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2h}(\overline{s}_{2h+1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{2h},\overline{a}_{2h})]\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{2h+1}(\overline{o}_{2h+1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{2h+1})\Big),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{2h-1}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{2h-1},\gamma_{2h-1})=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{2h-1}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{p}_{2h-1}}\mathbb{P}_{2h-1}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2h-1},\overline{p}_{2h-1}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h-1})\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2h-1}(\overline{s}_{2h-1},\gamma_{2h-1},\overline{p}_{2h-1}),

(C.9)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{2h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{2h},\gamma_{2h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{2h},\overline{p}_{2h},\overline{a}_{2h}}\mathbb{P}_{2h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2h},\overline{p}_{2h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2h})\mathds{1}[\overline{a}_{2h}=\gamma_{2h}(\overline{p}_{2h})]\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{2h}(\overline{s}_{2h},\overline{a}_{2h},\overline{p}_{2h}).

Definition C.10

(Strategy-dependent approximate common information model)
.

Given an expected approximate common information model
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
(as in Definition
C.7
) and
\overline{H}
joint strategies
g^{1:\overline{H}}
, where each
g^{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{1:\overline{H}}
for
h\in[\overline{H}]
(as in
Definition

C.3
),
we say
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is a
strategy-dependent expected approximate common information model
, denoted as
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H}})
, if it is consistent with the
strategy-dependent
beliefs
\{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{g^{h},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
(as per Definition
C.9
).

Definition C.11

(Length of approximate common information)
.

Given the compression functions
\{\text{Compress}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
, we define an integer
\widehat{L}>0
as the minimum length such that there exists a mapping
\widehat{f}_{h}:\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{\max\{1,h-\widehat{L}\}:h}\times\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\max\{1,h-\widehat{L}\}:h-1}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}
that satisfies: for each
h\in[\overline{H}+1]
and joint history
\{\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}\}
, we have
\widehat{f}_{h}(x_{h})=\widehat{c}_{h}
, where
x_{h}=\{\overline{o}_{\max\{h-\widehat{L},1\}},\overline{a}_{\max\{h-\widehat{L},1\}},\overline{o}_{\max\{h-\widehat{L},1\}+1},\cdots,\overline{a}_{h-1},\overline{o}_{h}\}
.

C-H
Main Results for Planning in QC LTCs

Theorem C.12

(Full version of
Theorem

IV.8
)
.

Given any QC LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
satisfying Assumptions
III.1
,
III.4
,
III.5
, and
III.7
, we can construct a Dec-POMDP problem
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
with SI-CIBs such that for any
\epsilon>0
, solving an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
can give us an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{L}
, and the following holds. Fix
\epsilon_{r},\epsilon_{z}>0
and given any
(\epsilon_{r},\epsilon_{z})
-expected-approximate common information model
\mathcal{M}
for
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
that satisfies Assumption
IV.7
, there exists an algorithm that can compute a
(2\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}+\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z})
-team optimal strategy for the original LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
with time complexity
\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|\cdot\texttt{poly}(|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|,|\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|,\overline{H})
. In particular, for any fixed
\epsilon>0
, if
\mathcal{L}
has a baseline sharing protocol as one of the examples in §
A
, one can construct such am
\mathcal{M}
and apply
Algorithm

1
to compute an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy for
\mathcal{L}
with the following complexities:

•

Examples 1, 3, 5, 6:

\texttt{poly}(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon})
;

•

Examples 2, 4, 7, 8:

\texttt{poly}(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})+2d},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon})
,

for some universal constant
C>0
. Recall that
\gamma
is the constant in
III.1
. And
d
is the delayed step of sharing, which is a constant as stated in §
A
. Note that if
d=\texttt{poly}\log H
, the complexity is still quasi-polynomial.

Proof.

We divide the proof into the following three
Parts
.

Part I:
Given any QC LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
satisfying Assumptions
III.1
,
III.4
,
III.5
, and
III.7
, we can construct a Dec-POMDP problem
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
with SI-CIBs such that finding an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy can give us an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{L}
, as shown in
Algorithm

1
.

Part II:
Given any
(\epsilon_{r},\epsilon_{z})
-expected-approximate common information model
\mathcal{M}
of the Dec-POMDP
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
, we aim to show that there exists an algorithm, Algorithm
6
, that can output an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
with
\epsilon=2\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}+\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}
. Then, together with the result in
Part I
, we can further get that the output of
Algorithm

1
is an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{L}
.

First, we need to prove that solving
\mathcal{M}
can get the
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
. We prove the following 2 lemmas first.

Lemma C.13
.

For any strategy
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
, and
h\in[\overline{H}]
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[|V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})|]\leq(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{r}+\frac{(\overline{H}-h+1)(\overline{H}-h)}{2}\epsilon_{z}.

(C.10)

Proof.

We prove it by induction. For
h=\overline{H}+1
, we have
V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})=V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=0
.

For the step
h\leq\overline{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[|V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})|]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}\left[|\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}}[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}){\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]}]-\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})|\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}\left[|\mathbb{E}_{\overline{z}_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})}[V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h}\cup\overline{z}_{h+1})]-\mathbb{E}_{\overline{z}_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{M},z}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})}[V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h}\cup\overline{z}_{h+1})]|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{r}+(\overline{H}-h)\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{1:h}\sim\overline{g}_{1:h-1}}||\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})-\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{M},z}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})||_{1}+\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{1:h}\sim\overline{g}_{1:h-1}}\left[|V_{h+1}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h+1})-V_{h+1}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h+1})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{r}+(\overline{H}-h)\epsilon_{z}+(\overline{H}-h)\epsilon_{r}+\frac{(\overline{H}-h)(\overline{H}-h-1)}{2}\epsilon_{z}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{r}+\frac{(\overline{H}-h)(\overline{H}-h+1)}{2}\epsilon_{z}.

In the third line of this proof, we had
\overline{z}_{h+1}\sim\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})
, where
\overline{z}_{h+1}
is generated as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\overline{z}_{h+1}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h}\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}},\overline{p}_{h}\in\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}}\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h+1}\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}},\overline{o}_{h+1}\in\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h+1}}\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{h+1}(\overline{s}_{h+1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h})\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{h+1}(\overline{o}_{h+1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h+1})\mathds{1}[\overline{\chi}_{h+1}(\overline{p}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{o}_{h+1})],

with
\gamma_{h}=\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]},\overline{a}_{h}=\gamma_{h}(\overline{p}_{h})
if
h=2k
, and
\overline{a}_{h}=\gamma_{h}
otherwise, for
k\in[H]
.
∎

Lemma C.14
.

Let
\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
be the strategy output by Algorithm
6
, then for any
h\in[\overline{H}],\overline{c}_{h}\in\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{h},\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
, it holds that

V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})\leq V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h}).

(C.11)

Proof.

We prove it by induction. For
h=\overline{H}+1
, we have
V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=0
.
For the timestep
h\leq\overline{H}
, we have

\displaystyle V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{M}}[\widehat{r}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})+V_{h+1}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h+1}){\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{h}]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{M}}[\widehat{r}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})+V_{h+1}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h+1}){\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\overline{g}_{h}]

\displaystyle\quad=Q_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h},\{\overline{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})

\displaystyle\quad\leq Q_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h},\{\widehat{g}_{j,h}(\overline{c}_{h},\cdot)\}_{j\in[n]})

\displaystyle\quad=V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h}).

For the first inequality, we use the induction hypothesis. For the second inequality, we use the property of
\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}
in algorithm and
V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})=V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{h})
. By induction, we complete the proof.
∎

We now go back to the proof of the theorem. Let
\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast}
be the solution output by Algorithm
6
, then for any
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}},h\in[\overline{H}],\overline{c}_{h}\in\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{h}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}\left[V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})\right]

(C.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}\left[\left(V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})\right)+\left(V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}\left[\left(V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})\right)+\left(V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{M}}(\overline{c}_{h})-V_{h}^{\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{c}_{h})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{r}+\frac{(\overline{H}-h)(\overline{H}-h+1)}{2}\epsilon_{z}+(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{r}+\frac{(\overline{H}-h)(\overline{H}-h+1)}{2}\epsilon_{z}

\displaystyle=2(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{r}+(\overline{H}-h)(\overline{H}-h+1)\epsilon_{z}.

For the first inequality, we use Lemma
C.14
. For the second inequality, we use Lemma
C.13
. Then, we can apply
h=0
to
Equation

C.12
and have
J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})\leq J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\ast})+2\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}+\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}
. Note that if
\mathcal{M}
(the model constructed in the Line
4
of
Algorithm

1
) satisfies
IV.7
, then
Algorithm

1
has time complexity
\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|\cdot\texttt{poly}(|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|,\overline{H})
. This is because there are at most
\overline{H}\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|
iterations in
Algorithm

6
(called in Line
5
of
Algorithm

1
), and for each iteration, it has
\texttt{poly}(|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}||\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|)
time complexity due to
IV.7
.
This completes the proof of
Part II
.

Part III:
If the baseline sharing of
\mathcal{L}
is one of the 8 examples in §
A
, we can construct an expected-approximate common information model
\mathcal{M}
of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
with
\epsilon_{r}=\epsilon_{z}=\epsilon
, and
\mathcal{M}
satisfies
IV.7
and is consistent with some belief.

We first prove the following lemmas, which are useful for bounding the errors
\epsilon_{r},\epsilon_{z}
.

Lemma C.15
.

Given any belief
\{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
such that an expected-approximate-common-information model
\mathcal{M}
is consistent with such belief, it holds that for any
h\in[\overline{H}],\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{h},\gamma_{h}\in\Gamma_{h}
:

\displaystyle||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},z}(\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})||_{1}

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1},

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[\mathcal{R}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}){\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}]-\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})|

\displaystyle\leq||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1},

where
\widehat{c}_{h}=
Compress
{}_{h}(\overline{c}_{h})
.

Proof.

Adapted
from Lemma 4 in
[
21
]
by changing the reward function of
r_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})
to
\mathcal{R}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{p}_{h})
. Note that the latter can still be evaluated given the common-information-based belief,
\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})
.
∎

Then, we define the belief states following the notation in
[
11
,
21
]
as
\overline{\bm{b}}_{1}(\emptyset)=\mu_{1}
,
\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}),\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})
, where
\overline{\bm{b}}\in\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
.
Also, for any
L\geq 1
, we define the approximate beliefs of states using the most recent
L
-step history as:

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-L}\sim\text{Unif}(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}),\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1})

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-L}\sim\text{Unif}(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}),\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1}).

Moreover, for any set
N\subseteq[n]
, we define
\overline{o}_{N,h}=\{\overline{o}_{i,h}\}_{i\in N}
. We can also define the beliefs of states given historical observations and actions as: for any
N\subseteq[n]
,

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-L}\sim\text{Unif}(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}),\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h}).

Let
S=|{\mathcal{S}}|
be the cardinality of the state space
{\mathcal{S}}
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma C.16
.

There is a constant
C\geq 1
such that the following hold. Given any LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
satisfying Assumption
III.1
, and let
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
be the Dec-POMDP after reformulation, strict expansion, and refinement. Let
\epsilon\geq 0
, fix a strategy
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
and indices
1\leq h-L<h-1\leq\overline{H}
. If
L\geq C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})
, then the following set of inequalities hold

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}\sim\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1})||_{1}\leq\epsilon

(C.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}\sim\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1})||_{1}\leq\epsilon

(C.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}\sim\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-L+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-L:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})||_{1}\leq\epsilon.

(C.15)

Proof.

Given any LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
, we can construct a Dec-POMDP
\check{\mathcal{D}}
such that the transition and emission functions of
\check{\mathcal{D}}
are the same as
\mathcal{L}
, and

f_{\tau,h-2d}=\{\overline{o}_{1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{1,1:h-2d-1},\{\overline{a}_{-1,2t-1}\}_{t=1}^{\lfloor\frac{h-2d}{2}\rfloor}\},f_{a}=\{\overline{a}_{1,h-2d:h-1},\{\overline{a}_{-1,2t-1}\}_{t=\lfloor\frac{h-2d}{2}\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor\frac{h}{2}\rfloor}\},f_{o}=\{\overline{o}_{1,h-2d+1:h},\overline{o}_{M}\}
,
we can then compute the common-information-based belief as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a},f_{o})\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h-2d}{\,|\,}f_{\tau,h-2d},f_{a},f_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a},f_{o})\frac{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a},f_{o}{\,|\,}f_{\tau,h-2d})}{\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h-2d}^{\prime},f_{a},f_{o}{\,|\,}f_{\tau,h-2d})}.

Denote the probability
P_{h}(f_{o}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a}):=\Pi_{t=1}^{2d}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{o}_{1,h-2d+t},\overline{o}_{M_{h-2d+t}}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},\overline{a}_{1,h-2d:h-2d+t})
, where
M_{h-2d+t}=\{(i,h-2d+t){\,|\,}(i,h-2d+t)\in M\}
denotes the set of observations at timestep
h-2d+t
and shared through additional sharing. With such notation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h-2d}{\,|\,}f_{\tau,h-2d},f_{a},f_{o})=

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}(\overline{o}_{1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{1:h-2d-1})(\overline{s}_{h-2d})P_{h}(f_{o}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a})}{\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}^{\prime}}\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}(\overline{o}_{1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{1:h-2d-1})(\overline{s}_{h-2d}^{\prime})P_{h}(f_{o}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d}^{\prime},f_{a})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}(\overline{o}_{1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{1:h-2d-1});f_{o})(\overline{s}_{h-2d}),

where
F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\cdot;f_{o}):\Delta({\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\Delta({\mathcal{S}})
is the posterior belief update function, which is defined as:
F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\overline{\bm{b}};f_{o})(\overline{s})=\frac{\overline{\bm{b}}(\overline{s})P_{h}(f_{o}{\,|\,}\overline{s},f_{a})}{\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}\overline{\bm{b}}(\overline{s}^{\prime})P_{h}(f_{o}{\,|\,}\overline{s}^{\prime},f_{a})}
for any
\overline{s}\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}},\overline{\bm{b}}\in\Delta(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}})
.

Then, we can define the approximate common information as
\widehat{c}_{h}:=\{\overline{o}_{h-2d-L+1:h-2d},\overline{o}_{1,h-2d+1:h},\overline{a}_{1,h-2d-L:h-1},\{\overline{a}_{-1,2t-1}\}_{t=\lfloor\frac{h-2d+1}{2}\rfloor}^{\lfloor\frac{h}{2}\rfloor},\overline{o}_{M}\}
and the corresponding approximate common information conditioned belief as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{a},f_{o})F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-2d-L+1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{h-2d-L:h-2d-1});f_{o})(\overline{s}_{h-2d}).

After that, we can construct
\{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},z}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\{\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
based on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
and
\{\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
. Now we verify that Definition
C.7
is satisfied.

•

By definition, the
\{\widehat{c}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
satisfies
Equation

C.5
.

•

For any
\overline{c}_{h}
and the corresponding
\widehat{c}_{h}
constructed above, according to Lemma
C.15
, we have:

\displaystyle||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq||F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}(\overline{o}_{1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{1:h-2d-1});f_{o})-F^{P_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot,f_{a})}(\overline{\bm{b}}_{h-2d}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{h-2d-L+1:h-2d},\overline{a}_{h-2d-L:h-2d-1});f_{o})||_{1}.

If we choose
L\geq C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})
, then for any strategy
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}
, by taking expectations over
f_{\tau,h-2d},f_{a},f_{o}
, from Lemma
C.16
and Lemma 12 in
[
21
]
, we have that for any
h\in[\overline{H}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1}\sim\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}}||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1}\leq\epsilon.

•

Based on
Lemma

G.2
,
\mathcal{M}
satisfies
IV.7
, since the condition
Turn-based structures
in §

is satisfied.

Therefore, based on Lemma
C.15
, such a model is an
(\epsilon_{r},\epsilon_{z})
-expected-approximate common information model with
\epsilon_{r}=\epsilon_{z}=\epsilon
and satisfies
IV.7
.

in §

. Then, the common information should be that for any

. We can compute the common-information-based belief as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})=

\displaystyle\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}_{h-2d}}\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-2d},f_{o})\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}_{h-2d}{\,|\,}f_{\tau,h-2d},f_{o})

Type 5:
Baseline sharing of
\mathcal{L}
is
Example 6
. Note that after reformulation, expansion, and refinement, the common information in
Example 6
is the same as that in
Example 1
. The only difference is in private information: for any
t\in[H-1]
,
i\in[n]
,
\overline{a}_{i,2t}\in\overline{p}_{i,2t+1}
always holds, and
\overline{a}_{i,2t}\in\overline{p}_{i,2t+2}
may happen. Meanwhile,
\overline{a}_{i,2t}\in\overline{c}_{2t+1}\subseteq\overline{c}_{2t+1}
always holds. Therefore, we can use the same expected approximate common information model as
Type 1
:
\forall t\in[H],\widehat{c}_{2t-1}=\{\overline{o}_{2t-L:2t-2},\overline{a}_{2t-1-L:2t-2}\},\widehat{c}_{2t}=\{\overline{o}_{2t-L:2t-2},\overline{a}_{2t-1-L:2t-1},\overline{o}_{N,2t-1}\}
. We construct the approximate common information conditioned belief as
\mathbb{P}_{2t-1}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2t-1},\overline{p}_{2t-1}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2t-1})=\overline{\bm{b}}_{2t-1}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{2t-L-1:2t-2},\overline{a}_{2t-1-L:2t-2})(\overline{s}_{2t-1})\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{2t-1}(\overline{o}_{2t-1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{2t-1}){}_{2t-1}^{1}(\overline{p}_{2t-1},\widehat{c}_{2t-1}),\mathbb{P}_{2t}^{\mathcal{M},c}(\overline{s}_{2t},\overline{p}_{2t}{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{2t})=\overline{\bm{b}}_{2t-1}^{\prime}(\overline{o}_{2t-L-1:2t-2},\overline{a}_{2t-1-L:2t-2},\overline{o}_{N,2t-1})(\overline{s}_{2t-1})\mathbb{P}_{2t-1}(\overline{o}_{-N,2t-1}{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{2t-1},\overline{o}_{N,2t-1}){}_{2t}^{1}(\overline{p}_{2t},\widehat{c}_{2t})
. Note that here we use the belief at timestep
2t-1
to construct
\mathbb{P}_{2t}^{\mathcal{M},c}
similarly as
Type 1
, and the functions
\{{}^{1}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
are defined as:
\forall t\in[H],\overline{p}_{2t-1},\widehat{c}_{2t-1},\overline{p}_{2t}
and
\widehat{c}_{2t}
,

\displaystyle{}_{2t-1}^{1}(\overline{p}_{2t-1},\widehat{c}_{2t-1})=\begin{cases}1&\text{the value of $\overline{a}_{2t-1}$ in $\overline{p}_{2t-1}$ is the same as the value of that in $\widehat{c}_{2t-1}$}\\
0&\text{o.w.}\end{cases}

\displaystyle{}_{2t}^{1}(\overline{p}_{2t},\widehat{c}_{2t})=\begin{cases}1&\text{for any $i\in[n]$ such that the random variable $\overline{a}_{i,2t-2}\in\overline{p}_{2t}$, }\\
&\text{the value of $\overline{a}_{i,2t-2}$ in $\overline{p}_{2t}$ is the same as that in $\widehat{c}_{2t}$ }\\
0&\text{o.w.}\end{cases}.

One can verify that Definition
C.7
is satisfied as in
Type 1
. This is because, for any
h\in[\overline{H}]
, compared to
Type 1
, the only difference is that there are only some actions in
\overline{p}_{h}
, and such actions will also appear in
\widehat{c}_{h}
. Therefore, if the values of such actions in
\overline{p}_{h}
are consistent with those in
\widehat{c}_{h}
, which is ensured by the functions
\{{}^{1}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
, then we can leverage the validation in
Type 1
.

and

One can verify that Definition

is satisfied as in

. This is because, for any

, the only difference is that there are only some actions of agent 1 in
\overline{p}_{h}

\widehat{c}_{h}

are consistent with those in

\{{}^{2}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}

.

Lastly, we can apply the result of
Part II
and obtain the time complexity as follows, and complete
Part III
:

•

Examples 1, 3, 5, 6:
Since the compressed common information satisfies
\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|=\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})}
, the complexity is
\texttt{poly}(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon})
;

•

Examples 2, 4, 7, 8:
Since the compressed common information satisfies
\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|=\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})+2d}
, the complexity is
\texttt{poly}(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})+2d},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon})
.

Combining
Parts I, II, III
, we complete the proof.
∎

C-I
Main Results for Learning in QC LTCs

Theorem C.17

(Full version of
Theorem

IV.9
)
.

Given any QC LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
satisfying Assumptions
III.1
,
III.4
,
III.5
, and
III.7
, we can construct a Dec-POMDP problem
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
with SI-CIBs.
Moreover, given any compression functions
\{\text{Compress}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
, evolution rules
\{\widehat{\phi}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
of the compressed common information
\{\widehat{c}_{h}\in\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1),\delta\in(0,1)
, and
\widehat{L}
as defined in
Definition

C.11
,
we can apply
Algorithm

2
with a universal constant
C
as chosen in
[
21
, Theorem 8]
. If the learned
K=2\overline{H}|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|
expected-approximate-common-information models
\{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})\}_{j\in[K]}
all satisfy Assumption
IV.7
, then
an
\epsilon_{0}
-team-optimal strategy for
\mathcal{L}
can be learned with probability
1-\delta
,
with time and sample complexities polynomial in the parameters of
\{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})\}_{j\in[K]}
,
where
\epsilon_{0}
is defined as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}:=\min_{j\in[K]}~~2\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))+2\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))+\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Specifically, if
\mathcal{L}
has a baseline sharing protocol as one of the examples in §
A
, then given any
\epsilon\in(0,1),\delta\in(0,1)
, we can construct compression functions
\{\text{Compress}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
and evolution rules
\{\widehat{\phi}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]}
, such that
Algorithm

2
can learn an
\epsilon
-team optimal strategy of
\mathcal{L}
with probability
1-\delta
, with the following time and sample complexities:

•

Examples 1, 3, 5, 6:

\texttt{poly}\left(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|}{\epsilon})},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log(\frac{1}{\delta})\right)
;

•

Examples 2, 4, 7, 8:

\texttt{poly}\left(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}(|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)^{C\gamma^{-4}\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}|}{\epsilon})+2d},|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\overline{H},\frac{1}{\epsilon},\log(\frac{1}{\delta})\right)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into the following three
Parts
.

Part I:
Given any LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
satisfying the assumptions in the theorem, we can construct a
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
by the reformulation, strict expansion, and refinement of
\mathcal{L}
. According to Theorem
IV.4
, we know that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
has SI-CIBs with respect to the strategy space
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}
.

Part II:

Given any LTC problem
\mathcal{L}
, we can apply Algorithm
2
for learning in such a problem. For any
j\in[K]
, let
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{j,\ast}
be the output of
Algorithm

6
(Line
11
of
Algorithm

2
), we can guarantee that

J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{j,\ast})\geq\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})-2\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))-2\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))
according to the result of
Part II
of the proof of
Theorem

C.12
. Meanwhile, let
\widehat{j}
be the index of the strategy selected by Algorithm Pos-Dec (Line
13
), such that
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\widehat{j},\ast}
is the output of Pos-Dec. Then, we can adapt
[
21
, Lemma 21]
to the team setting and guarantee that

\displaystyle\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})-J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\widehat{j},\ast})\leq\min_{j\in[K]}\left(\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})-J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{j,\ast})\right)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}

with probability
1-\delta_{3}\geq 1-\delta
. Combining these two results, we can guarantee that
\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})-J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\widehat{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{\widehat{j},\ast})\leq\epsilon_{0}
with probability
1-\delta
.
Also, together with
Proposition

IV.1
and
Theorem

IV.4
, with probability
1-\delta
, we conclude that the output of
Algorithm

2
is an
\epsilon_{0}
-team-optimal strategy of
\mathcal{L}
.

Meanwhile, we analyze the sample and time complexities of
Algorithm

2
. For Algorithm BaSeCAMP (Line
8
), from
[
21
, Lemma 19]
, it has time and sample complexities
(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}||\overline{\mathcal{A}_{h}}|)^{\widehat{L}}\log(\frac{1}{\delta_{2}})
; for Algorithm LEE (Line
10
), it has sample complexity
\overline{H}N_{0}
and time complexity
\texttt{poly}(\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\Gamma_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|)
; for Algorithm Pos-Dec (Line
13
), it has sample and time complexities
KN_{2}
; for Algorithm
6
(Line
11
), as long as the models
\{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})\}_{j\in[K]}
satisfy
IV.7
, it has time complexity
\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{h}|\cdot\texttt{poly}(|\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|,\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}||\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|,\overline{H})
. Therefore,
Algorithm

2
has time and sample complexities polynomial in the parameter of
\{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})\}_{j\in[K]}
.

Part III:
If the baseline sharing of
\mathcal{L}
is one of the examples in §
A
, we can construct the compressed common information with length

,

with

. Here
\widehat{L}=L
.

The compression functions

) can guarantee that the output

\displaystyle\forall\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}},|V_{0}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\emptyset)-V_{0}^{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}},\widehat{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})}(\emptyset)|\leq\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))+\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j}))+\epsilon_{apx}^{j}

with probability

, with

\displaystyle\epsilon_{apx}^{j}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{1}+2\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|\frac{\zeta_{1}}{\zeta_{2}}+\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{h}|\theta_{2}+\frac{\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{h}|^{2\widehat{L}}\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}|\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{h}|^{\widehat{L}}}{\phi}

\displaystyle\qquad+\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}\max_{\overline{g}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}\mathds{1}[h>\widehat{L}]\cdot 2\cdot d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g^{h,j})),

, and for any set
X\subseteq\overline{{\mathcal{S}}},d_{{\mathcal{S}},h}^{g,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(X)=\sumop\displaylimits_{\overline{s}\in X}d_{{\mathcal{S}},h}^{g,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s}),\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g):=\{\overline{s}\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}{\,|\,}d_{{\mathcal{S}},h}^{g,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{s})<\phi\}
.
Note that

here is the expected approximate common-information model with components
\{\widehat{c}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\{\widehat{\phi}_{h}\}_{h\in[\overline{H}]},\Gamma
, and
\forall j\in[K],\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j})

.

\displaystyle\epsilon_{apx}^{j}\leq 4\epsilon_{1}+\max_{h\in[\overline{H}]}\max_{\overline{g}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}\mathds{1}[h>\widehat{L}]\cdot 2\cdot d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g^{h,j})).

Also, we can prove the following lemma.

Lemma C.18
.

Given any
\widehat{L}>0
and parameters
K,\alpha,\beta,\epsilon_{1},N_{0},N_{1},O,S
specified in
Algorithm

2
, and let
\{g^{1:\overline{H},j}\}_{j\in[K]}
be the output of the algorithm BaSeCAMP(
\widehat{L},N_{0},N_{1},\alpha,\beta,K
) (Line
8
in
Algorithm

2
). As long as
\widehat{L}\geq C\frac{\log(\overline{H}SO/(\epsilon_{1}\gamma))}{\gamma^{4}}
, where
C
is a large enough constant as chosen in
[
21
, Theorem 8]
, then with probability at least
1-\delta_{2}
, there exists at least one
j^{\ast}\in[K]
such that for any strategy
\overline{g}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}},g\in\mathcal{G}_{1:\overline{H}},h\in[\overline{H}],N\subseteq[n]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{g}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1})||_{1}\leq\epsilon_{1}+\mathds{1}[h>\widehat{L}]\cdot 6\cdot d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{g}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1})||_{1}\leq\epsilon_{1}+\mathds{1}[h>\widehat{L}]\cdot 6\cdot d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overline{g}}||\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h-1},\overline{a}_{1:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})-\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{g}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h})||_{1}\leq\epsilon_{1}+\mathds{1}[h>\widehat{L}]\cdot 6\cdot d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g)),

\displaystyle d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{\overline{g},\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{U}_{\phi,h-\widehat{L}}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(g^{h,j^{\ast}}))<\epsilon_{1},

where
\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{g}(\cdot):=\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{apx,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,d_{{\mathcal{S}},h-\widehat{L}}^{g,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}})
, and
\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{apx,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}
is defined as follows:
\forall D\in\Delta(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}),N\subseteq[n]

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{apx,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},D)=\mathbb{P}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-\widehat{L}}\sim D,\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1})

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{apx,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1},D)=\mathbb{P}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-\widehat{L}}\sim D,\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1})

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{apx,\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h},D)=\mathbb{P}(\overline{s}_{h}=\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{s}_{h-\widehat{L}}\sim D,\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h-1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1},\overline{o}_{N,h}).

Proof.

We can adapt Lemma 18 and Lemma 19 in
[
21
]
to our setting. Note that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
only has
\gamma
-observability at odd timesteps
h=2t-1,t\in[H]
.
Therefore, it holds that
\overline{\bm{b}}_{h+1}(\overline{o}_{1:h+1},\overline{a}_{1:h})=\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}(\overline{o}_{1:h},\overline{a}_{1:h-1})
and
\overline{\bm{b}}_{h+1}^{g}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h+1},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h})=\overline{\bm{b}}_{h}^{g}(\overline{o}_{h-\widehat{L}+1:h},\overline{a}_{h-\widehat{L}:h-1})
, since the communication action at timestep
h=2t-1
(i.e.,
\overline{a}_{h}=m_{t}
) does not affect the state, and
\overline{s}_{h+1}=\overline{s}_{h},\overline{o}_{h+1}=\emptyset
. A similar relationship of the beliefs also holds for the other two types of beliefs. We can thus apply the corollary.
∎

Therefore, based on Lemma
C.18
, we have
\max_{\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}\in\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{1:\overline{H}}}J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}})-J_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{j^{\ast},\ast})\leq 2\big(\overline{H}\epsilon_{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}))+\overline{H}^{2}\epsilon_{z}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}))+\frac{6\epsilon}{200(\overline{H}+1)^{2}}\big)
, where
j^{\ast}
is the
j
selected in Lemma
C.18
, and
\overline{g}_{1:\overline{H}}^{j^{\ast},\ast}
is the strategy computed in the
j^{\ast}
-th iteration of Line
11
of
Algorithm

2
.

Now, we validate for each example in §
A
that, under the compressed common information,
\epsilon_{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}))
and
\epsilon_{z}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}))
in
Equation

C.21

can be made small. Note that after reformulation, expansion, and refinement,
Examples 1
and
5
are the same, and
Examples 2
and
7
are the same.
Therefore, all the examples in §

can be classified into the following 6
Types
.
Finally, from Lemma

, for any
h\in[\overline{H}]

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}[\mathcal{R}_{h}(\overline{s}_{h},\overline{a}_{h},\overline{p}_{h}){\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h}]-\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{h}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}})}(\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})|\leq||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}),c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1}

(C.22)

\displaystyle||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}),z}(\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h},\gamma_{h})||_{1}\leq||\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\overline{c}_{h})-\mathbb{P}_{h}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(g^{1:\overline{H},j^{\ast}}),c}(\cdot,\cdot{\,|\,}\widehat{c}_{h})||_{1}.

(C.23)
